Remarks.&mdash;During July the highest reading of the barometer was 30.064, on the 4th; and the lowest 29.610, on the 20th. The highest temperature was 96&deg;.s, on the 1st; and the lowest 740, on the 1st. s.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 24th, averaging 8.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Kain fell on 18 days, measuring 7.24 inches. During August the highest reading of the barometer was 30.090, on the 14th; and the lowest 29.772, on the 1st. The highest temperature was 98&deg;, on the 15th; and the lowest 750, on the 27th. s.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 18th, averaging 9.1 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 20 days, measuring 4.14 inches. During September the highest reading of the barometer was 30.176, on the 22nd; and the lowest 29.612, on the 19th. The highest temperature was 970, on the 17th; and the lowest 67&deg;, on the 30th. n.e. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 18th, averaging 16.5 miles an hour during 24 hours. WLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1892.
192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [jULY-SEPT.,. (lieceived 18th October 1892. j CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, iith October 1892. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1892, July-September. b. The Custom House (^L Ml 11) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium {Hk.Th 30 and Hk.Th 80 per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the 1st July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon ($i 7jC 1&#39;^), Changchow (J| ^|.|), Fotochow (ff, HI &sect;[]), and Kowloon (jl fl) stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.&mdash;The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:&mdash; i&deg;. CAPSUIMOON. JUNKS. July. August.
